{{wide,text-align:center
## Alpha Rules Release © 2026 23BLUENINJA - All Rights Reserved
# Beyond the Prism

}}

This document is playtest material for Beyond the Prism, a tabletop roleplaying game. All content is subject to change.

## Attributes

In BtP, five **Attributes** represent your capabilities as an individual, described to the right.

Attributes are measured in **Attribute Points**, each of which increases the size of your **Mod Die** (short for Modifier Die) for that Attribute, as shown in the table below. An Attribute with 0 points has no Mod Die.

::

##### Attribute Points & Modifier Dice
:
{{column-count:2

| Points | Die  |
|:------:|:----:|
|   1    | 1d2  |
|   2    | 1d4  |
|   3    | 1d6  |
|   4    | 1d8  |
|   5    | 1d10 |
|   6    | 1d12 |

| Points |  Die  |
|:------:|:-----:|
|   -1   | -1d2  |
|   -2   | -1d4  |
|   -3   | -1d6  |
|   -4   | -1d8  |
|   -5   | -1d10 |
|   -6   | -1d12 |
}}

::

{{background:darkblue,padding:1em,margin-bottom:0.5em
#### Rolling d2s
The simplest way to roll a d2 is by rolling your favorite die, counting an odd roll as a 1 and an even roll as a 2. Ie, you can roll 1d6, treating 1, 3, and 5 as 1, and 2, 4, and 6 as 2.

When you roll a d2 in Tandem with another Modifier Die, be sure to use a different sized die for the d2 so you don't get them confused.
}}

::

Each Attribute Point beyond 6 add +1 to Checks with that Attribute. When something refers to the number of points you have in a given Attribute, it will use the symbol of that Attribute. For example, heavy armor allows you to add your $[VIG] Points (Vigor Points) to your character’s AC.

There is a limit to how high your Attributes can be, determined by your level. The limit starts at 3 points, and rises with your level as shown in the table below.

::

##### Attribute Limit
:
| Level | Attribute Point Cap |
|:-----:|:-------------------:|
|   1   |          3          |
|   4   |          4          |
|   10  |          5          |
<!--|   16  |          6          |-->

\column

### $[VIG] Vigor (VIG)
Vigor measures the physical strength and health of your character.
:
**Affects** :: encumbrance, grappling, jumping, melee weapons, throwing, and AC with Heavy armor.
**Grants** :: one *Vitality* per 2 points and *Temporary Endurance* on a Long Rest.
**Saves** :: against smashing, pushing, proning, and Arctic, Somatic, and Sonic damage.

### $[AGI] Agility (AGI)
Agility measures how quickly and easily your character can move their body.
:
**Affects** :: Deft weapons, hiding, landing, and AC with Light armor.
**Increases** :: *Movement* by total points each point (+1 at 1, +2 at 2, etc).
**Saves** :: against grappling, proning, fast-moving hazards, and Arctic, Thermic, Caustic, and Galvanic damage.

### $[PER] Perception (PER)
Perception measures how well your character can react to what they experience with their bodily senses.
:
**Affects** :: Initiative, Precise weapons, Ranged weapons, and AC with Medium armor.
**Increases** :: $[PER] Radius by total points each point (+1 at 1, +2 at 2, etc).
**Saves** :: against ambushes, instantly-occurring hazards, and Thermic, Somatic, Caustic, and Galvanic damage.

### $[INT] Intelligence (INT)
Intelligence measures your character's ability to learn, and how well they apply their knowledge.
:
**Affects** :: aptitude with connecting, recalling, and understanding information.
**Grants** :: one Hobby per point and one Profession per 2 points.
**Saves** :: against mental assault, illusions, and Psychic and Cosmic damage.

### $[PRS] Presence (PRS)
Presence measures your character’s ability to influence the actions of others.
:
**Affects** :: aptitude with influencing, lying, performing, and manipulating.
**Grants** :: one Hobby involving communication with people per point and one *Radiance* per 2 points.
**Saves** :: against domination, emotional manipulation, and Psychic, Chthonic, Divine, and Profane damage.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Checks & Modifiers
When your character attempts to affect the world in a way that success is not guaranteed, such as attacking a creature, picking a lock, or even cooking an omelette, the PM will call for a **Check**. Checks are executed in the order below.

1. Identify any modifiers to your Check.

2. Roll a d20 and the relevant Mod Die, if you have one.

3. Add the numbers together.

4. Compare the Check Total to the DC.

5. Determine the result.

6. Resolve the effect.

### The d20
You will typically roll a d20 anytime a Check is called for. This roll represents what **Fate** feels the result should be. Rolling a 1 on the d20, referred to as a **Natural 1**, is especially damning. Rolling a 20, referred to as a **Natural 20**, represents outstanding success.

### Modifier Dice & Types of Checks
When making a Check, the activity being performed determines the **Mod Die** you will use. There are four types of checks, described below.
:
* **Attack Checks** are made when you attempt to harm a specific creature or object. For example, swinging a weapon.

* **DC Checks** are special Checks made when you attempt to affect an area, forcing one or more other entities to make a Save Check.

* **Save Checks** are made when you attempt to avoid a dangerous situation or effect, such as a trap or a hostile spell.

* **Skill Checks** are made when you attempt to do anything not covered by the previous Checks, such as lifting a log, recalling knowledge, or interpreting body language.

### Rolling in Tandem
Many facets will allow you to roll two Mod Dice in **Tandem**, meaning you roll both and use the higher number as the value of your Mod Die for that Check. If one and only one of the Mod Dice is negative, instead subtract that die's result from the other.

\column

### Advantage & Disadvantage
You will sometimes roll a Check with Advantage or Disadvantage, representing a situation in your favor, or against you.

:

**Advantage** :: means you roll two d20s and take the higher value for that Check.

**Disadvantage** :: means you roll two d20s and take the lower value for that Check.

Multiple effects granting Advantage or Disadvantage to a Check do not stack; a Check either has Advantage, Disadvantage, or neither. Having both Advantage and Disadvantage cancels both, no matter how many instances of either are being applied.

### Bolstered & Hindered
When facet or effect says your Check is **Bolstered** or **Hindered**, that means you increase or decrease the size of the largest Mod Die for that Check by a number steps equal to the Bolstered or Hindered level, respectively. Bolstered and Hindered levels subtract from each other, so if a Check would be Bolstered 2 and Hindered 1, the Check is Bolstered 1 as a result.

For example, if you make an Attack Check with a d10 Mod Die that is Bolstered 2, you would roll a d12 for the Mod Die and add 1 to the Check Total. If you make a Save Check with a **d4** Mod Die that is Hindered 4, you would reduce the size of the Mod Die by 4 steps, down to a **-d4**.

If multiple effects would add Bolstered or Hindered levels, add their levels together. If a Check would be Bolstered past a **d12** Mod Die, add the remaining Bolstered levels to the Check Total. If a Check would be Hindered past a **-d12** Mod Die, subtract the remaining Hindered levels from the Check Total.

### Empowered & Weakened
Certain circumstances or effects may cause your Checks to be **Empowered** or **Weakened**, increasing your chance of success or failure, respectively.

:

**Empowered** :: means you either roll an extra **d4**, adding the result to the value of your Mod Die (**not** the Check Total), or if the Check is being rolled in Tandem with two Mod Dice that are both **d4s** or larger, add their results together for the value of your Mod Die.

**Weakened** :: means you roll an extra **d4** and subtract the result from the value of your Mod Die.

Like Advantage and Disadvantage, multiple effects causing a check to be Empowered or Weakened do not stack, and they cancel each other out.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

### Resolving Checks

#### Step 1: Apply Modifiers
After the PM calls for a Check, identify any effects that would cause it to be Empowered, Weakened, or made at Advantage or Disadvantage. If the Check is Bolstered or Hindered, adjust the size of your Mod Die.

#### Step 2: Roll the Dice
Roll your d20 alongside any Mod Dice you have for the Check.

#### Step 3: Calculate the Check Total
Add the value of the d20 to the value of your Mod Die. The result is your **Check Total**. Rarely, you may have other modifiers to apply at this stage, such as excess Bolstered levels.

#### Steps 4: Compare to the DC
Announce your Check Total to the PM, who compares it against the **DC** (Difficulty Class) of the Check. The DC varies depending on what the Check is for. If your Check Total is equal to or greater than the DC, your character Succeeds. You might already be aware of the DC, or you may have to ask the PM if you Succeeded. DCs are often purposely hidden to elevate suspense at the table.

#### Step 5: Measure your Success
**Success** and **Failure** don't end at the DC. When a Check Total is 5 or more above or below the DC, the result of the Check is often greater than a normal Success or Failure.

### Attack Checks
When your PC attempts a harmful action against a specific entity, they will make an Attack Check. The Mod Die you use and the DC for the Check are determined by the kind of attack you are making. For example, a Gladiator attacking with a longsword would use their Martial Die against the AC of their target, while an Arcanist casting a spell would use their Arcana Die against one of the target’s Attribute DCs (10 plus the target’s Attribute Points). The result of an Attack Check is usually damage being dealt to something.

Beating the DC of an Attack Check by 5 or more is called a **Deadly Success**, while failing by 5 or more is called a **Complete Failure**.

\column

#### Weapon Attack Checks
Weapon Attack Checks refer to Attack Checks made with a weapon, most commonly with the Strike Action. The DC of a Weapon Attack Check is usually the target's AC.

#### Spell Attack Checks
Spell Attack Checks refer to Attack Checks called for by a spell. Spell Attack Checks are usually made against an Attribute of the target, meaning the DC equals 10 plus the Attribute Points the creature has for that Attribute.

### DC Checks
When your PC does anything that affects an area (called an **Area of Effect**, or AOE) such as casting a spell that makes a line of flame, they will make a DC Check, which is a special Check made by rolling only your Mod Die, then adding 10 to the result. Each affected creature then makes a Save Check using the Attribute specified in the effect. DC Checks can have Advantage or Disadvantage for the purpose of other effects, such as *Radiance*, but are unaffected by those modifiers.

### Save Checks
When your PC is targeted by a harmful effect or faced with a dangerous situation, such as a giant boulder falling from the ceiling, they will make a Save Check. A Save Check always specifies the Attribute it targets, meaning you use that Attribute’s Mod Die as the Mod Die for the Check. When you make your character, the Class you choose will list certain Attribute Save Checks you can roll in Tandem with your Class Die.

The DC of a Save Check is determined by the effect that caused it. Beating the DC of a Save Check by 5 or more is called a **Complete Success**, and often results in avoiding any harmful effects entirely, while failing by 5 or more is called a **Deadly Failure**, with deadly effects to match.

### Skill Checks
When your PC tries to interact with the world in any way not covered by the other Checks, they will make one of the three types of Skill Checks: **Attribute**, **Basic**, or **Trained**. The DC of a Skill Check is determined by the PM, as are the effects of Success and Failure, including **Complete Success** and **Complete Failure**, if your roll at least 5 above or below.


![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

{{wide
| $[VIG] VIG / AGI $[AGI] | $[VIG] VIG / PER $[PER] | $[VIG] VIG / INT $[INT] | $[VIG] VIG / PRS $[PRS] | $[AGI] AGI / PER $[PER] |
|:-------:|:-------:|:-------:|:-------:|:-------:|
| Play a physical sport | Throw an object | Carefully apply force | Physically intimidate or impress | Land a difficult jump |
| Sprint | Climb a wall     |   |                               | Hide                  |

}}

{{wide
| $[AGI] AGI / INT $[INT] | $[AGI] AGI / PRS $[PRS] | $[PER] PER / INT $[INT] | $[PER] PER / PRS $[PRS] | $[INT] INT / PRS $[PRS] |
|:-------:|:-------:|:-------:|:-------:|:-------:|
| Quickly solve a puzzle | Perform a dance | Detect a lie | Manipulate or insult someone | Lie or threaten |
|   |                 | Deduce from evidence |Interact with an animal      |Persuade with reason|

}}

#### Attribute Skill Checks
When your PC performs an action based solely on their mental or physical capability, you will make an Attribute Check. The PM determines the Attribute you will use, such as asking for a Perception Check to spot a tiny object. When you make an Attribute Check, use your Attribute Die as the Mod Die for that Check.

#### Basic Skill Checks
Basic skill checks are made when your PC attempts to do something that most people can learn without specialized instruction or significant trial and error. A Basic Skill Check is always made with two Attribute Dice rolled in Tandem. For example, if you attempt to hide from a guard, the PM can ask you to roll a Tandem $[AGI] / $[PER] check, representing how fast you find acceptable cover and how well you conceal yourself from sight.

In the table above you’ll find Attribute combinations that cover many common PC endeavors.

#### Trained Skill Checks
Trained skill checks are only made when a PC attempts to perform an action that one of their **Hobbies** or **Professions** allows them to do. They are made by rolling your Mod Die for the relevant Hobby or Profession in Tandem with the Mod Die of the most relevant Attribute for the Check. For example, if you have the Fisherman Profession and you are trying to catch a fish, your PM can ask for a Tandem Fisherman/ $[PER] Check, representing your experience as a fisherman and your ability to feel when you have a bite.

\column

Skill Checks involving your class are also considered Trainined Skill Checks, and use your Class Die in Tandem with an Attribute Die.

### Critical Failure & Success

#### Critical Failure
Rolling a Natural 1 on any Check is a Critical Failure, causing the Check to be Weakened.

#### Critical Success
Rolling a Natural 20 on Check means you score a **Critical Success**. Critical Successes have different effects depending on the type of Check, as detailed below, but scoring a Critical Success always restores one expended *Radiance*.

:

**Weapon Attack Checks** :: that score a Critical Success inflict a Condition based on the type of damage dealt, as described in the Strike Action.

**Spell Attack Checks** :: that score a Critical Success are automatically a Deadly Success, and the spell’s level increases by 1 when resolving the Deadly Success effects.

**Save Checks** :: that score a Critical Success are an automatic Complete Success, and the effects of that Complete Success for the creature become nothing, no matter what they would have been.

**Skill Checks** :: that score a Critical Success have the value of their Mod Die set to the maximum value.


### Difficulty Classes

Below is a table showing a range of DCs used for Checks, and the difficulty they represent.

{{wide



##### DC Difficulties

|    |              |     |                                                                                d6 Success                                                                                                                           |
| DC |  Difficulty  | Use |                                                                                Chance                                                                                                                               |
|:--:|:------------:|:----|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------:|
| 3  | Minimal      | The minimum DC, used for circumstances that have an extremely small chance of failure, but for which failure can be highly significant.                                                                             | 99% |
| 5  | Easy         | This DC should be used when the character is highly likely to succeed, but has a slim chance of failure. Most low-stakes Checks should use this DC.                                                                                                           | 95% |
| 10 | Engaging     | This is the base DC for things that are reasonably challenging. A PC with a d6 Mod Die has a 73% chance of success against this DC.                                                                                 | 73% |
| 15 | Hard         | This DC represents a serious challenge where success is no longer the likely outcome for most creatures.                                                                                                            | 48% |
| 20 | Extreme      | The DC is for highly dangerous or challenging situations where the PC will most likely fail, but still has a significant chance at success.                                                                         | 23% |
| 25 | Irrational   | This DC is impossible to achieve without at least a d6 Mod Die, and even then represents a nearly impossible situation.                                                                                             | 3%  |
| 30 &ensp;&ensp;| Impossible &ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp;  | This DC should only be used when the creature with an opportunity to make the check has at least a d10 Mod Die, and represents the barest chance of success for such creatures. | 0% &ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp;&ensp; |
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Hobbies & Professions
Rather than a static list of skills such as athletics or stealth, BtP uses Hobbies and Professions to represent the skills your PC has, gained from what they did to survive and thrive before their adventuring career.

When a PC attempts something that requires specific, intentional instruction or practice (i.e. picking someones pocket, riding a horse, translating a language, or recalling specialized information), they must have exposure to that activity or information through one of their Hobbies or Professions in order attempt a Skill Check. If they don't, the PC has no chance of succeeding in the way they would like to.

### Hobbies
A Hobby is a single activity that your PC became capable of through practice in their spare time. Each Hobby has a corresponding Mod Die that you roll as the Mod Die for any Skill Check you make using that Hobby.

### Professions
In addition to their Hobbies, your PC also has one or more Professions. A Profession is the occupation your character had before becoming an adventurer; what they did to make money or otherwise survive. Professions can provide access to a wide array of potential Skill Checks, and they allow your character to make money during Downtime.

### Ranks & Leveling up
Your PC's skill at each of their Hobbies and Professions is measured by their **Rank**, which determines the size of their Mod Die. Starting at level 3 and every odd level after, you can increase the rank of one of your Hobbies or Professions by one step, or you can become a Novice in a new Hobby. The highest rank you can have in either is limited by your level, as shown below.

::

##### Hobby & Profession Ranks
:
{{column-count:2
| Bonus |    Rank    |
|:-----:|:-----------|
|  1d4  | Novice     |
|  1d6  | Amateur    |
|  1d8  | Proficient |
|  1d10 | Adept      |
|  1d12 | Master     |

| Level |  Rank Cap  |
|:-----:|:-----------|
|   1   | Proficient |
|   5   | Adept      |
|   9   | Master     |
}}

\column

### Guidelines for Hobbies & Professions

You can choose almost anything for your Hobbies and Professions, so long as your PM agrees to it. Horseback riding, smithing, sleight-of-hand, basket weaving, anything! You can also work with your PM to identify some Hobbies that may be relevant to the adventure ahead. Infinite options can be daunting however, so a list of backgrounds are provided later in this document for inspiration or ready-made selection.

#### Choosing Hobbies
When deciding on your PC's Hobbies, keep the following in mind.
:
- ***Specific.*** Hobbies should be written down on your character sheet as a specific activity, usually in gerund form (ending in "-ing").

- ***Amateur.*** Having a Hobby implies that your PC is practiced at that activity, but not to the degree that they could monetize it successfully.

- ***Narrow.*** When choosing a broad activity, such as reading or art, you must narrow it down to a specific genre or category. For example, "Nonfiction Reading" or "Oil Painting".

- ***Weapon Training.*** When you gain a Hobby, you can choose to replace it with the training to wield a specific weapon, such as a longsword or a flail.

#### Choosing Professions
When deciding on your PC's Professions, keep the following in mind.
:
- ***Official.*** Professions should be written down on your character sheet as formal occupations, such as "Blacksmith", "Farmer", or "Accountant".

- ***Sustaining.*** Having a Profession means your PC has all the skills required to make money or otherwise feed themselves with their given profession.

- ***Broad Experience.*** A PC with the "Fishing" Hobby may be able to catch and identify a fish, but a PC with the "Fisherman" Profession can catch fish, read weather patterns, understand deck logs, identify fish diseases, and any number of other things required to run a fishing business.

- ***Logical Training.*** If you choose a Profession that would naturally grant you an ability to wield weapons or wear armor, such as “Soldier”, your PM will decided which weapons and armor you gain the training to use. You don’t add your Class Bonus to your AC unless your Class is what allows you to wear your armor.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Actions
When you want to interact with the world or story, you do so by having your PC take an **Action**. Actions are things your PC can do that have defined rules. Most Actions have a **Cost**, usually measured in $[AP] (**Action Points**, or AP). You must spend the $[AP] listed in an Action’s cost to perform it. Some Actions have **Requirements** you must meet to use them as well.


### $[AP] Action Points (AP)
When you roll **Initiative**, you gain $[AP] equal to your $[AP] Maximum, which is 5 in most cases. You can perform Actions on your turn by spending $[AP] as listed in the Action’s Cost. You regain expended $[AP] at the end of your turn.

Outside an **Encounter**, you generally don’t track $[AP].

#### Temporary Action Points
Some facets can grant temporary $[AP], which is not constrained by your $[AP] maximum and typically disappears at the end of the turn.

### Reactions & Triggers
When an Action's cost includes the **Reaction** tag (for example “Reaction, 1 $[AP]”), it will also list a **Trigger**. Triggers can be any set of circumstances, and you can only use a Reaction when the Trigger occurs. You can only use one Reaction in response to a given Trigger, even if you have multiple Reactions that share the same Trigger.

### Consecutive Action Penalty (CAP)
Many Actions have a stacking penalty if they are used multiple times in the same turn, known as a **CAP** (Consecutive Action Penalty). The CAP of an Action can vary, but the most common is a **Basic CAP**, meaning the Action costs an additional 1 $[AP] for each time it has been used that turn (0 on the first use each turn).

### Subsequent Actions
When an Action requires you to perform another Action as part of its execution, you don’t spend $[AP] for the subsequent Action. However, you can't take an Action if you can't perform its subsequent Actions. For example, being Restrained prevents you from taking Move Actions, meaning you can't take any Action that tells you to Strike or Stride.

### Improvised Actions
If you want your PC to do something not covered by an existing action, the PM will arbitrate if its possible, the $[AP] you need to spend, and what kind of Check you need to make.

\column

### Basic Action Reference

Basic Actions are available to all creatures. The table below lists all the Basic Actions for easy reference. These Actions are fully defined further down.

::

##### Basic Actions
|    Name     | Description                  |
|:------------|:-----------------------------|
| Assist      | Help another creature.       |
| Block       | Block a weapon attack.       |
| Crawl       | Crawl on the ground.         |
| Detect      | Perceive sounds or smells.   |
| Drop Prone  | Drop to the ground.          |
| Escape      | Attempt to escape restraint. |
| Hide        | Attempt to hide from sight.  |
| Interact    | Interact with an object.     |
| Jump        | Cross gaps or gain height.   |
| Prepare     | Take an Action off-turn.     |
| Search      | Look for something.          |
| Seize       | Grab a creature.             |
| Shove       | Push a creature.             |
| Sprint      | Run as fast as you can.      |
| Stabilize   | Attempt to save a creature.  |
| Stand       | Get up from prone.           |
| Step        | Carefully move one space.    |
| Stride      | Move up to your *Movement*   |
| Strike      | Make a physical attack.      |
| Sustain     | Maintain an effect.          |
| Take Cover  | Duck for cover.              |

::

### Action Categories
Each Action lists one or more categories that may affect how they function or are responded to, detailed below.
:
* **Basic** Actions are available to all creatures.

* **Concentrate** Actions require focus to execute.

* **Hostile** Actions will be seen as a sign of aggression to non-Allied creatures.

* **Manipulate** Actions involve touching or moving objects.

* **Move** Actions  involve moving the body a significant amount.

* **Secret** Actions are rolled by the PM on behalf of the Player, so that the Player does not know the result.

#### Exclusive Categories
The categories listed below are only available to certain classes. Your class will tell you which categories it grants you access to.
:
* **Arcane** Actions are available to those that manipulate Light with their own power.

* **Combat** Actions are available to those trained in combat.

* **Supernatural** Actions are available to those capable of evoking Light from other entities.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Action List
Below are rules for each Action your PC can take.

### Basic Actions
Basic Actions are available to all creatures.

#### Assist
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic
**Cost** :: Reaction, 2 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: The creature accepts your Assist
**Trigger** :: A creature you can see makes an Attack or Skill Check while you are within sufficient range to Assist, as determined by the PM.
}}

You attempt to aid a creature. Explain to the PM how you plan to Assist. If you feel one of your Hobbies or Professions would be relevant to the triggering Check, you can attempt to justify its use. If the PM agrees to your method of assistance and that you are within sufficient range, you grant your ally a benefit based on the type of Check.

:

{{left
**Attack Check** ::  The creature has Advantage on the Check.
**Attribute Skill Check** :: The creature can roll your Attribute Die in Tandem with theirs.
**Basic or Trained Skill Check** :: The creature can replace one of their Tandem Mod Dice with one of your Mod Dice, based on how you are Assisting.
}}

#### Block

**Categories** :: Basic, Concentrate, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: Varies
**Requirements** :: You are wielding a Blocking weapon.

Spend any amount of $[AP]. Until the start of your next turn, your AC gains a **Blocking Bonus** against a number of Attack Checks made against your AC equal to the $[AP] spent. You can choose which Attack Checks to add your Blocking Bonus against, which is based on the weight Property of your Blocking weapon and the attacking weapon. You also gain Crushing Resistance equal to the Blocking Bonus against any Attack Check you Block.

::

##### Blocking Bonus by Weapon Weight
:
|           | Massive          | Heavy          | Other          |
| Attacking | Blocking         | Blocking       | Blocking       |
|----------:|-----------------:|---------------:|---------------:|
| Light     | Twice Deadly DV  | DV + Deadly DV | Deadly DV      |
| Normal    | DV + Deadly DV   | Deadly DV      | Deadly DV      |
| Heavy     | Deadly DV        | Deadly DV      | DV             |
| Massive   | DV               | DV             | DV             |


\column

#### Crawl
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 1 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You are Prone and have at least 2 *Movement*
}}
While Prone, you move 1 space, or a number of spaces up to your $[AGI] Points minus your Slowed level.

#### Detect

**Categories** :: Basic, Secret
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP], Basic CAP

You attempt to detect subtle sounds or smells. State what you are trying to Detect. The PM makes a Secret $[PER] Check on your behalf, at Disadvantage if you are trying to Detect something outside your $[PER] radius.

:

{{left
**Success** :: You successfully sense what you were trying to Detect, if such a thing exists.
**Complete Success** :: As a Success, and the PM tells you the direction the stimulus is originating from.
**Failure** :: You sense irrelevant stimuli, or nothing at all.
**Complete Failure** :: You misinterpret the stimuli around you, giving you false information.
}}

#### Drop Prone

**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 1 $[AP]
**Requirements** :: You aren’t Prone or Restrained

You drop to the ground, becoming Prone.

#### Escape
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You are Immobilized, Restrained, or Seized
}}
Choose a single source imposing the Immobilized, Restrained, or Seized condition on you. Make a $[VIG] Check against a DC determined by the chosen source.

:

{{left
**Success** :: You end the Immobilized, Restrained, and Seized conditions imposed on you by the chosen source.
**Complete Success** :: As a Success, and you can move 1 space if you are no longer Immobilized by anything.
**Failure** :: Nothing.
**Complete Failure** :: Escaping again before the end of your next turn costs an additional 2 $[AP].
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Hide

**Categories** :: Basic, Secret
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP]

You attempt to conceal yourself from detection. There must be sufficient cover between you and creatures that could see you in order to Hide from them. The PM makes a Secret Tandem $[AGI] / $[PER] check on your behalf and notes the Check Total, which serves as the DC for the $[PER] Check of any creature that attempts to Detect you while you remain hidden from them.

If you are within the $[PER] Radius of a creature whose $[PER] DC is equal to or above your Check Total, they sense you automatically.

When you take a Move Action other than Stepping or Crawling, you must Hide again before the end or your turn if you want to continue avoiding detection, and the Check is made at Disadvantage if you are within 1 space of the last place you Hid.

#### Interact

**Categories** :: Basic, Manipulate
**Cost** :: 0 $[AP], Basic CAP

You interact with your environment, typically with your hands. Use this action when you want to draw or stow an object or weapon, open a door, or pick something up. Based on your Interaction, the PM may call for a Check to determine your success, or deem it impossible, such as drawing a weapon while Restrained. Complex acts may require you to Interact multiple times, as determined by the PM.

#### Jump
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 1 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You aren’t Prone and have at least 2 *Movement*.
}}
You leap from your space, using the rules below depending on the type of jump you are making.

:

{{left
**Long Jump** :: If you haven’t moved this turn, you jump 1 space. If you have, you jump a number of spaces equal to 1 plus the number of spaces moved in a straight line prior to Jumping, to a maximum of 2 plus your $[VIG] points.

**High Jump** :: You jump up, reaching a number of spaces above you equal to 1 plus half your $[VIG] points, rounded down. Creatures with negative $[VIG] cannot jump high enough to be significant. You can also move 1 space horizontally as part of this jump to land on an elevated surface.
}}

\column

#### Prepare

**Categories** :: Basic, Concentrate
**Cost** :: Varies

You prepare an Action to perform at an opportune moment. Chose any Action you could perform and spend the $[AP] required to perform it (including its CAP). You then state a Trigger you would like to Prepare for. If the stated Trigger occurs before the start of your next turn, you can spend 1 $[AP] to take the chosen Action as a Reaction (no further $[AP] required).

Preparing an Action counts as taking it for the purpose of its CAP. You can Prepare multiple Actions, but you can only execute one for a given Trigger. You can Prepare Actions that cost 0 $[AP], such as Interacting to release your grasp on an object, but you must still spend the 1 $[AP] required to respond to your Trigger.

#### Search

**Categories** :: Basic, Secret
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP], Basic CAP

You visually scan the area in a cone with a size equal to your $[PER] Radius. State what you would like to search for. The PM then makes a Secret $[PER] Check on your behalf.

:

{{left
**Success** :: You see what you're Searching for, or confirm that it isn’t visible from your vantage point.
**Complete Success** :: As a Success, but if what you’re Searching for isn’t visible the PM gives you a hint to its location if it’s within the cone.
**Failure** :: You don’t see what you’re Searching for.
**Complete Failure** :: You think you see what you’re Searching for.
}}

#### Seize
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP]
**Requirements** :: You have an empty hand and are not Restrained
}}
You attempt to grab hold of a creature. Make a Tandem $[VIG] / $[AGI] Check against the creature’s choice of their $[VIG], $[AGI], or $[PER]. If you use two hands, the Check is Bolstered 1.

:

{{left
**Success** :: The target is Seized by you.
**Complete Success** ::The target is Seized by you, and you may knock them Prone.
**Failure** :: Nothing.
**Complete Failure** :: You are Buffered 1.
}}

Alternatively, you may latch on to a creature larger than you. When you do, you become Seized until you let go, and you move with the creature when it does. Each time the creature takes a Move Action, you must

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

make a $[VIG]  Save Check against a DC of 10 or the number of spaces the creature moved, whichever is larger. On a Failure, you are flung from the creature after they finish moving, landing in a space adjacent to you.

#### Shove

**Categories** :: Basic, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP]
**Requirements** :: You are not Immobilized

You attempt to push over a creature. Make a Tandem $[VIG] / $[AGI] Check against the creature’s choice of their $[VIG], $[AGI], or $[PER].

:

{{left
**Success** :: You move the target 1 space directly away from you.
**Complete Success** :: You move the target 1 space directly away from you and they fall Prone.
**Failure** :: Nothing.
**Complete Failure** :: You are Buffeted 1 until the start of your turn.
}}

#### Sprint
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: 5 $[AP]
**Requirements** :: You aren’t Prone and have at least 2 *Movement*.
}}
You dedicate your body and senses to moving as fast as possible. Choose a type of *Movement* available to you. You move any number of spaces up to your 4 times your *Movement* for that type.

When you Sprint, you can’t take non-Move Actions that turn, and if your AC is calculated with your $[AGI] or $[PER], those Attributes Points are subtracted from your AC until the start of your next turn.

#### Stabilize
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Concentrate, Manipulate
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You can touch a creature you can see with the Dying condition.
}}
You attempt to stop a creature from dying. Make a Tandem $[PER] / $[INT] Check against a DC of 10 plus the creature’s Dying level. You may replace one of the Attribute Mod Dice with the Mod Die of a relevant Hobby or Profession if applicable.

:

{{left
**Success** :: The creature’s Dying level reduces by 1
**Complete Success** :: The creature’s Dying level reduces by 2.
**Failure** :: Nothing.
**Complete Failure** :: If you aren’t using a Hobby or Profession, the creature’s Dying level increases by 1.
}}

\column

#### Stand
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 1 $[AP]
**Requirements** :: You are Prone and have at least 2 *Movement*.
}}
You stand up and are no longer Prone.

#### Step
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You aren’t Prone and have at least 2 *Movement*.
}}
You Step carefully to avoid danger, moving 1 space on the ground. You can Juke with this move if able. Stepping doesn’t trigger Reactions that respond to Move Actions like Reaction Strikes.

You can’t Step into a space of Treacherous Terrain.

#### Stride

**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 1 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You aren’t Prone

Choose a type of *Movement* available to you. You move any number of spaces up to your *Movement* for that type. You can break up your movement between other Actions, including Striding with a different type of *Movement*.

#### Strike

**Categories** :: Basic, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP]

You attack your target. If you’re using a Ranged weapon, make a $[PER] Attack Check against the AC of a creature or object you can sense within range of your weapon. Otherwise, make a $[VIG] Attack Check against a creature or object you can sense within 1 space of you. Add the value of your Mod Die to the damage dealt.

:

{{left
**Success** :: The target takes damage equal to your weapon’s DV of a type the weapon can deal.
**Deadly Success** :: The target takes damage equal to your weapon’s Deadly DV of a type the weapon can deal. If your Check Total is 10 or more above the DC, add your weapon’s DV to the damage dealt.
**Critical Success** :: As a Deadly Success, and the target suffers one of the below effects based on the damage type dealt.

- **Crushing** the target is Bruised.

- **Piercing** the target loses 1 Vitality.

- **Slashing** the target is Bleeding 1, or its Bleeding level increases by 1.

- **Other** The damage dealt increases by your weapon's Deadly DV.

:
**Failure** :: Nothing.
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

##### Unarmed Strikes
When you Strike without using a weapon, you make an Unarmed Strike using your body as a melee weapon, which has the Close-Up property, a DV of 0, and a Deadly DV equal to your $[VIG] Points.

#### Sustain
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Concentrate
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP], or the $[AP] listed in parentheses in the duration
**Requirements** :: The effect you’re Sustaining did not begin this turn, has not been Sustained this turn, and has not reached its maximum duration
}}
You maintain an ongoing effect for a previous Action. Any of your Sustained effects that didn’t begin on your current turn end if you don’t Sustain them before the end of your turn.

#### Take Cover
{{left
**Categories** :: Basic, Move
**Cost** :: 2 $[AP]
**Requirements** :: You are within 1 space of Light Cover or a Medium or larger object and aren't Immobilized
}}
You duck for cover. Choose the thing you are Taking Cover behind and make a DC 10 $[PER] Check. On a Success, if you chose Light Cover it becomes Full Cover. If you chose an object, it becomes Light cover. This benefit lasts until you take a Move Action.

### Arcane Actions
Arcane Actions are available to those that manipulate Light with their own power.

#### Countercast
{{left
**Categories** :: Arcane, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: Reaction, 2 $[AP], Basic CAP; The non-$[AP] resources normally required for you to cast a spell of the same level.
**Trigger** :: A creature you can sense within your $[PER] Radius casts an arcanum of opposing polarity to a leveled spell you know or have Prepared (defined below).
}}
You throw prisma of an opposing polarity at the caster in an attempt to cancel out their spell. Make a Spell Attack Check against a DC of 10 plus the level of the spell and the caster.

:

{{left

**Success**          :: The spell is prevented, and the resources spent to cast it are wasted.
**Complete Success** :: As a Success, and you regain half the non-$[AP] resources you spent, rounded down.
**Failure**          :: The level of the triggering spell is reduced by 1 (minimum level 1).
**Complete Failure** :: Nothing.
}}

\column

##### Opposing Polarity Arcana
:
{{column-count:2
|       |    |       |
|------:|:--:|:------|
| Fire  | <> | Ice   |
| Mind  | <> | Bio   |
| Scour | <> | Arc   |
| Blood | <> | World |
| World | <> | Moon  |
| Moon  | <> | Blood |
}}

::

#### Deflect

**Categories** :: Arcane, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: Reaction, 1 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Trigger** :: An Attack Check is made against your AC.

You quickly form any Light you can into a minor barrier. Make a Spell Attack Check against the triggering Attack Check Total. The Attack Check penalty is reduced by 1 if the Attack Check is with a Heavy weapon, or by 2 for a Massive weapon.

:

{{left
**Success:** :: The triggering Attack Check Total decreases by 3.
**Deadly Success:** :: The triggering Attack Check Total decreases by 5.
**Failure:** :: The triggering Attack Check Total decreases by 2.
**Complete Failure:** :: Nothing.
}}

#### Identify Arcane
**Categories** :: Arcane, Concentrate, Secret
**Cost** :: 5 $[AP]

Choose an object or area you can sense within 1 space of you that you suspect is enchanted or imbued with Light that you have not attempted to Identify since your last Long Rest. The PM makes a Tandem $[PER] / $[INT] Check on your behalf against a DC of 10 plus the level of the arcane effect, if applicable.

:

{{left
**Success** :: You learn the level of the effect and its type (Fire, Mind, etc), if applicable.
**Complete Success** :: As a Success, and you learn how to activate the effect, if applicable. The PM may elect to tell you additional information.
**Critical Success** :: As a Complete Success, and you learn exactly what the arcane effect does, and how to dispel it if applicable.
**Failure** :: You learn the type of the effect, if applicable.
**Complete Failure** :: Nothing.
**Critical Failure** :: You get a false sense of the type, even if there is no arcane effect.
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Imbue
**Categories** :: Arcane, Concentrate, Manipulate
**Cost** :: 5 $[AP]

You imbue Light into an object you can touch. Perform an Action available to you that casts an arcanum. The Action isn’t Hostile, and instead of a spell being cast, the object is imbued with an amount of Light equal to the level of the spell.

When you Imbue a gemstone, it gains 1g of Charge per level of the spell, up to the gem’s weight. For example, if you cast a 1st-level arcanum into a 5g citrine, it becomes a 1g Charged Citrine. You can further Imbue the Charged Citrine with up to 4 more spell levels.

### Combat Actions
Combat Actions are available to those trained in combat.

#### Defend
{{left
**Categories** :: Combat, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: Reaction, 1 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Requirements** :: You aren’t Prone and have at least 2 Movement
**Trigger** :: You sense a creature within your $[PER] Radius become the target of an Attack Check or a $[VIG], $[AGI], or $[PER] Save Check.
}}
You attempt to defend a creature from harm. Move a number of spaces up to half your *Movement*. If you move to a space between the targeted creature and the source of the Check, you become the target of the Check.

#### Reaction Strike
{{left
**Categories** :: Combat, Move, Hostile
**Cost** :: Reaction, 1 $[AP], Basic CAP
**Trigger** :: A creature within reach of a weapon you’re wielding takes a Manipulate action, makes a Ranged Attack Check, or would move to a space out of your reach during a Move Action it’s taking.
}}
You Strike the creature. On a Deadly Success, if the creature took a Manipulate Action, it's prevented, and the $[AP] it spent is wasted.

\column

### Supernatural Actions
Actions are available to those capable of evoking Light from other entities.

#### Identify Supernatural
**Categories** :: Supernatural, Concentrate, Secret
**Cost** :: 5 $[AP]

Choose an object or area you can sense within 1 space of you that you suspect is corrupted, cursed, extraplanar, hallowed, possessed, or otherwise afflicted by extraplanar forces that you haven't attempted to Identify since your last Long Rest. The PM makes a Tandem $[PER] / $[PRS] Check on your behalf against a DC of 10 plus the level of the supernatural affliction, if applicable.

:

{{left
**Success** :: You learn the level of the affliction and its general type, if applicable.
**Complete Success** :: As a Success, and you learn the planar origin of the affliction, if applicable. The PM may elect to tell you additional information.
**Critical Success** :: As a Complete Success, and you learn exactly what the supernatural affliction is, and how to dispel it if applicable.
**Failure** :: You learn the general type of affliction, if applicable.
**Complete Failure** :: Nothing.
**Critical Failure** :: You get a false sense of an affliction, even if there is nothing supernatural about the target.
}}

#### Suppress
**Categories** :: Supernatural, Hostile
**Cost** :: Reaction, 2 $[AP], Basic CAP; The non-$[AP] resources normally required for you to cast a spell of the same level.
**Trigger** :: A creature you can sense within your $[PER] Radius casts an evocation of a type you could learn or Prepare.

You call upon your supernatural endowment to overpower the triggering evocation. Make a Spell Attack Check against a DC of 10 plus the level of the spell and the caster.

:

{{left
**Success** :: The spell is prevented, and the resources spent to cast it are wasted.
**Deadly Success** :: As a Success, and you regain half the non-$[AP] resources you spent, rounded down.
**Failure** :: The level of the triggering spell is reduced by 1 (minimum level 1).
**Complete Failure** :: Nothing.
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Exploration
Different environmental factors can affect how you interact with your surroundings.

### Brightness
There are four levels of brightness that determine how well you can see: **Full Brightness**, **Low Brightness**, **Darkness**, and **Absolute Darkness**.

#### Full Brightness
While in Full Brightness, such as daylight or a well-lit room, you can see normally. Sources of Full Brightness emit double their radius in Low Brightness.

#### Low Brightness
While in Low Brightness, such as an enclosed space lit by a candle, your $[PER] Checks that rely entirely on sight have Disadvantage unless you have **Night Vision**.

#### Darkness
While in Darkness, such as night under an open sky, you are considered Blinded for any Check you make in relation to something that is also in Darkness, unless you have Night Vision. You are only Blinded for the purposes of those Checks however, and do not suffer the other effects of the Blinded Condition.

You can perceive areas within your line of sight according to the brightness level of that area.

#### Absolute Darkness
In Absolute Darkness, such as a sealed chamber with no light sources, all creatures that rely on sight to perceive their environment are Blinded.

### Visibility
Sometimes other factors such as fog or intense rain may also impede your ability to see. There are three levels of visibility: **Clear Visibilty**, **Low Visibility**, and **Minimal Visibility**.

#### Clear Visibility
Clear Visibility means nothing is impacting visibility in your environment, and you can see as normal.

#### Low Visibility
Low Visibility is caused by things like heavy fog or rain. While in Low Visibility conditions, you cannot visually sense anything beyond twice your $[PER] Radius.

\column

#### Minimal Visibility
Minimal Visibility is caused by extreme conditions such as sandstorms, raging blizzards, or intense whipping rain. While in Minimal Visibility conditions, you cannot visually perceive anything beyond your $[PER] Radius, and within your $[PER] Radius, you can only see the silhouette of creatures and objects. Attack Checks against the $[PER] of creatures in areas of Minimal Visibility have Advantage, while $[PER] Attack and Save Checks made by those creatures have Disadvantage.

### Objects

An object is any physical entity that is not a creature. They have stat blocks just like creatures, and can be targeted in the same way. All objects share statistics from the stat block below, and will not repeat information that does not vary based on its characteristics. Objects do not have a level, and their $[VIG] is based on their Material and Size.

::

{{statBlock,border:"2px outset red"
{{statBlockTop
{{stats

::

#### Object
*< Size > Object, < Material >*

**Armor Class**  :: 3 (immobile)
**Endurance**    :: Varies by Size

**Movement**    :: 0
**Senses**      :: Perception Radius 0

**Resistances** :: Varies by Material
**Vulnerabilities** :: Varies by Material
**Immunities**  :: Cosmic, Cthonic, Psychic, Somatic

}}

\column

{{attributes

::

-5 (5) $[AGI]
:
-5 (5) $[PER]
:
-5 (5) $[INT]
:
-5 (5) $[PRS]

}}
}}



##### Traits

**Object** :: The object has no AP and cannot gain AP.
::
}}


![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}
