## Encounters
When time is of the essence, you will enter the Encounter mode of play. During an Encounter, you will track how you spend $[AP] on your turn and between it.

### Initiative
An Encounter (most commonly a fight) begins when the PM calls for Initiative. When they do, you roll a special $[PER] check, called an Initiative Check. The result of this check determines when you take your turn relative to other creatures in the Encounter.

#### Surprise
If the PM determines that any participant in the encounter is taken by surprise, that creature has Disadvantage on their Initiative Check.

#### Tied Initiative
If two PCs tie in their Initiative, the one with the larger $[PER] Mod Die (counting Bolstered and Hindered levels) will go first. If their Mod Dice are the same size, the PCs will decide amongst themselves who goes first.

### Acting in an Encounter
To perform an Action during an Encounter, you must spend $[AP] (Action Points). When you roll Initiative, you gain $[AP] equal to your maximum (5). You regain all spent $[AP] at the end of your turn. Most Actions can only be performed on your turn. Actions with the **Reaction** tag in their Cost can be performed any time their Trigger occurs.

Actions and $[AP] are explained in depth in the Actions section of this document.

### Movement
*Movement* is a resource that determines the number of spaces you can travel on the ground when you take the Stride action. Outside of an Encounter, your *Movement* is doubled when Striding.

#### Movement Types
Some facets will give you the ability to move in different ways, like in the air, water, or up vertical surfaces. Each additional movement type you gain will have its own base *Movement* value. Like your regular *Movement*, this is increased by your total points each point of one of your Attributes, though not necessarily $[AGI] (+1 at 1, +2 at 2, etc).

Whenever a rule or facet refers to your *Movement* without specifying a type, it is referring to your normal movement, which is how far you can move along the ground. Your PM may allow you to use a different movement type for the effect.

There are five movement types beyond normal ground movement, listed in the table below.

::

#####  Movement Types
|        |                                          | Associated           |
|  Name  | Description                              | Attribute            |
|:------:|:----------------------------------------:|:--------------------:|
| Burrow | Digging through loose ground.            | $[VIG]               |
| Climb  | Climb vertical surfaces without a Check. | $[VIG] or $[AGI]     |
| Fly    | Flying through the air.                  | $[VIG] or $[AGI]     |
| Swim   | Swimming through liquid.                 | $[VIG] or $[AGI]     |
| Warp   | Teleporting to a visible space.          | $[INT] or $[PRS]     |

::

#### Treacherous Terrain
The PM may declare that particularly difficult, unstable, or narrow terrain is considered **Treacherous Terrain**. Moving into or out of a space considered as Treacherous Terrain consumes 2 spaces of *Movement*.

For example, if you attempt to cross a narrow ledge, or move through a knee deep river, you would need to expend 2 *Movement* for every space you travel. If you only have 1 *Movement* left, you can’t move into or out of Treacherous Terrain.

### Falling
When you begin falling, you immediately fall 5 spaces. For every 1 $[AP] spent while falling, you fall another 5 spaces plus the last amount of spaces you fell after the Action or effect you spent $[AP] on resolves.

For example, if you begin falling on another creatures turn, you will immediately fall 5 spaces. If you then spend 1 $[AP] to Interact with a piece of your equipment, you will fall 10 spaces once that Action is resolved. If you spend another 1 $[AP] to do anything else, you will fall 15 spaces. If you then spend 2 $[AP] on a single action, such as Interacting with something else, you will fall 20 spaces, then 25 spaces.

The next time your turn ends, if you have fallen less than 100 spaces since the end of your last turn, you fall the remaining amount of spaces it would take to get you to 100. If you are still falling, you immediately fall another 5 spaces at the start of the next turn, and the cycle repeats. You can only fall a maximum of 100 spaces before the end of each of your turns.

When you fall more than 1 space, you take crushing damage equal to the number of spaces you fell upon landing. If you are Prone when you fall, roll a d20. On a 1, the damage you take is doubled. On a 20, it is halved.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Landing on a Creature or Object

If you land on something after falling, and the fall would deal damage, you and the thing you fell on take half the damage you would have taken. If the thing you fell on has Crushing Resistance, you take the amount of damage resisted by it.

### Moving on a Grid
BtP uses a triangular grid for Encounters. If your PM uses this grid, use the following rules.

#### Spaces
Each point where lines intersect represents a space. When you move from one space to another, you do so by moving along any of the lines connecting your current space to an adjacent space.

#### Range
When measuring the range between two entities, start counting from a space adjacent to one of the entities and stop counting in the space occupied by the other using the shortest route.

#### Size
All creatures and objects have a Size, which affects how many spaces they take up on a grid. Most PCs will be Medium or Small, but other creatures can get much larger or smaller. Medium and smaller creatures only occupy one space, and multiple Tiny and Miniscule entities can occupy the same space.

On a triangular grid, each Size above Medium occupies all spaces adjacent to those occupied by the next-smallest Size. This allows the spaces occupied by each Size to be measured via radius from their center space, as shown in the table below.

::

##### Large Creature Spaces
|Size|Spaces (Radius)|
|:-:|:-:|
|Large|1|
|Gigantic|2|
|Enormous|3|
|Gargantuan|4|
|Colossal|5|
|Titanic|6|

::

The PM can increase the size radius of Titanic creatures past 6 as they see fit.

#### Juking
When moving on a triangular grid, if you have at least 5 *Movement*, you can hop across the line between two spaces (the base of any triangle formed with your space as the top) to the space in straight line from your current position. This is known as **Juking**, and only costs 1 space of *Movement*. 

You can Juke once each time you take a Move Action that allows you to move. You can Juke even if there are Hostile creatures occupying the spaces at both ends of the line you move through, but you can't Juke into or out of Treacherous Terrain.

### Damage
When a creature takes damage, their *Endurance* is reduced by the amount of damage taken. For example, if a creature with 12 *Endurance* takes 5 Thermic damage, they will have 7 *Endurance* remaining. Effects that deal damage will usually state the exact amount of damage the target takes based on the result of a Check. You will then often add the value of your Mod Die to that number, as with Striking with a Weapon. Damage always has a type, and when a creature or object would take damage, you first have to apply any Immunity, Resistance, or Vulnerability the target has to the damage types being dealt.

#### Halving
Damage is often halved based on the result of a Check. Spells typically deal half damage on a Failed Attack Check or Successful Save Check. If you would add your Mod Die to a damage amount that was halved, it is usually added after doing so, unless otherwise stated by the effect.

Damage is the most common thing to be halved, but in general, the default rule when halving anything in BtP is to **round up**. Any effect that needs to round down will say so specifically.

#### Immunity
Entities can have **Immunity** to one or more damage types. If something would take damage of a type it has Immunity to, it instead takes no damage. Entities can also have Immunity to Conditions. If you would gain a Condition you are immune to, you instead don't, though you can still be targeted by effects that cause the condition.

#### Resistance
Most entities have Resistance to one or more damage types. Damage Resistances have a level, and each time something takes damage of a type it has Resistance to, the damage is reduced by that level. For example, if a creature with Thermic Resistance 3 takes 5 Thermic damage, it would be reduced by 3, and the creature would take 2 damage. Resistances to the same damage type from different sources add together.

Entities can also have Resistance to Conditions, which work similarly and reduce the level of the received condition by the level of their Resistance to it.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Vulnerability

Some entities have **Vulnerability** to one or more damage types. Damage vulnerabilities are displayed with a die value. Each time something takes damage of a type it has Vulnerability to, the **Vulnerability Dice** are rolled and added to the total damage taken. For example, when a creature with Arctic Vulnerability 1d4 takes Arctic damage, the damage is increased by 1d4.

### Cover
There are two tiers of cover that you can take advantage of during an Encounter, **Light Cover** and **Full Cover**. The PM determines what counts as cover and what doesn’t.

#### Light Cover
Light Cover gives you Advantage on $[VIG], $[AGI], and $[PER] Save Checks, so long as the cover is between you and the origin of the effect forcing you to make the Check. Attack Checks made against you also have Disadvantage if there is Light Cover between you and the attacker.

Light Cover includes things like large fallen logs, benches, and overturned tables. Allies in a direct path between you and a Hostile creature also count as Light Cover if they are no more than one size smaller than you.

#### Full Cover
Full Cover gives you an automatic Complete Success on $[VIG], $[AGI], and $[PER] Save Checks, so long as the cover is between you and the origin of the effect forcing you to make the Check. Attack Checks made against you are also an automatic Complete Failure if there is Full Cover between you and the attacker.

Full Cover is anything sturdy enough to blunt an impact that completely obscures your PC from a source of harm.


### Areas of Effect
Many things can create an Area of Effect, causing multiple creatures to be affected at once.

#### Circle
Listed as "X-space circle", each creature on the ground within X spaces of the space chosen as the center of the circle will be affected.

#### Cone
Listed as "X-space cone", each creature between two straight lines, each X-spaces long and 60 degrees apart starting from the cone's origin will be affected. A visual representation of a 3-space cone at different orientations is shown below. Creatures in the red-dotted spaces would not be affected.

::

![](https://i.imgur.com/7DAKMq4.png){width:100%}

::

#### Cylinder
Listed as "Cylinder (X-space radius, Y spaces high)", effects with this area behave as as an X-space circle, but creatures in the circle no more than Y spaces above the origin space are also affected.

#### Emanation
Listed as "X-space emanation", creatures within X spaces of the emanation's origin in any direction will be affected. Emanations are persistent and move with their origin.

#### Line
Listed as "X-space line", each creature in a line drawn X spaces from the origin space will be affected. Some effects will require the line be straight, but others can create lines the connect any sequence of adjacent spaces.

#### Sphere
Listed as "X-space-radius sphere", each creature within X spaces of the origin space in any direction will be affected.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Radiance
*Radiance* is a resource that represents the light within your PC, and how brightly it shines. Your PC starts with 1 *Radiance* and gains an additional *Radiance* for every 2 $[PRS] points they have. You regain spent *Radiance* equal to half your $[PRS] points when you finish a Long Rest. When you score a Critical Success on any Check, you regain one spent *Radiance*. If you have all your *Radiance*, you can immediately use that *Radiance* on the Check, before determining the result.
:
You can spend *Radiance* to do one of the following.
:
- ***Advantage.*** Gain Advantage on any Check you make.

- ***Empower.*** Empower any Check you make with Advantage, including one you spent *Radiance* to gain Advantage on.

- ***Unconventional Skill Check.*** Use a Hobby or Profession for any Skill Check, regardless of how relevant it would be.

- ***Bonus Damage.*** Deal bonus damage on a Weapon Attack equal to the weapon's Deadly DV.



## Endurance and Vitality
Life and death in BtP are managed by *Endurance* and *Vitality*. These together represent the overall state of your PC's health, and your ability to keep moving.

### Endurance
*Endurance* represent your PC's ability to keep functioning under high stress situations. Your PC's *Endurance* is gained from their Class. When your PC is dealt damage, the damage is subtracted from their current *Endurance*.

#### Temporary Endurance

Many effects can grant you ***Temporary Endurance***, which you track separate from your regular *Endurance*. Notably, when you finish a Long Rest, you gain *Temporary Endurance* equal to your $[VIG] Points times your Class Bonus.


When you take damage, reduce your *Temporary Endurance* first if you have any. *Temporary Endurance* lasts until you finish your next Long Rest, unless the effect that granted it states a duration. If you ever gain *Temporary Endurance* while you already have some, choose whether you keep your current amount or gain the new amount, including the new duration. *Temporary Endurance* never stacks.

\column

### Vitality
Your PC's physical body and how close they are to death is represented by your *Vitality*, which starts at 3. If your *Vitality* ever drops to 0, you die. *Vitality* is lost when you take damage while at 0 *Endurance*. The amount of damage doesn't matter, if you take damage with 0 *Endurance*, you lose 1 *Vitality*.

### Dying
When you lose *Vitality*, you gain the **Dying** Condition with a level equal to the amount of *Vitality* you are missing. Dying forces you to make **Survival Saves**, described below. If your *Endurance* is above 0 while Dying, you also take Somatic damage equal to twice your Dying level at the start of your turn. Somatic Resistance cannot reduce this damage below 1. You can Stabilize yourself on your turn to attempt to reduce your Dying level. The Dying condition is also removed entirely when you regain any *Vitality*.

### Survival Saves
When your *Endurance* hits 0, and when you start your turn with the Dying condition, you must make a special $[VIG] Save Check referred to as a Survival Save. The DC for a Survival Save equals 10 plus your Dying level.

If you have any *Endurance* when you make a Survival Save, you do so at Advantage.

:

{{left
**Success** :: You fall Unconscious.
**Complete Success** :: Your Dying level reduces by 1, and you wake up if you are Unconscious, so long as another effect is not keeping you Unconscious.
**Critical Success** :: As a Complete Success, and you regain 1 *Endurance* if you have none.
**Failure** :: You fall Unconscious and your Dying level increases by 1.
**Deadly Failure** :: You fall Unconscious and lose 1 *Vitality*. After gaining the Dying condition from losing your *Vitality*, your Dying level increases by 1.
**Critical Failure** :: As a Deadly Failure, but you lose 2 Vitality instead of 1.
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Resting
When your PC is low on resources, you can take a Rest to regain strength.

### Short Rests
A Short Rest is achieved when your PC spends 10 minutes without performing an Action that costs more than 1 $[AP]. At least 5 minutes must also be spent occupying the same space in a resting position (sitting, leaning against a wall, etc).

When your PC finishes a Short Rest, they gain the following benefits.
:
- ***Vitality.*** They regain missing *Vitality* equal to half their $[VIG] points (remember, **always round up** in BtP).

- ***Facet.*** Uses Any facets that state they regain uses or are otherwise refreshed on a Short Rest are restored.

- ***Endurance.*** You can spend **Rest Charges** to regain *Endurance* equal to half your Endurance Maximum. Your PC has a number of Rest Charges equal to their maximum *Vitality*. 

A PC can only benefit from two Short Rests per Long Rest, and not within an hour of each other.

### Long Rests
A Long Rest is achieved when your PC spends at least 6 hours sleeping and 2 hours without performing an Action that spends non-$[AP] resources.

When your PC finishes Long Rest, they gain the following benefits.
:
- ***All Endurance.*** They regain all missing *Endurance*.

- ***Temporary Endurance.*** They gain *Temporary Endurance* equal to their $[VIG] points times their Class Bonus.

- ***Rest Charges.*** They regain all spent Rest Charges.

- ***Vitality.*** They regain missing *Vitality* equal to 1 plus half their $[VIG] points.

- ***Radiance.*** They regain spent spent *Radiance* equal to half their $[PRS] points.

\column

{{note
#### Heroic & Gritty
The Resting Rules are the easiest way to modify the stakes and difficulty of your game. The rules to the left are the default, Heroic rules. If you want a Gritty game, remove regaining *Vitality* on a Short rest, and reduce the *Vitality* regained on a Long Rest to 1 or half the PC's $[VIG] points, whichever is higher.
}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

{{descriptive,columns:2,wide

{{wide,text-align:center

## Damage Types
BtP has fourteen damage types, categorized by rarity into ranks. Higher ranked damage types are more rare, and Immunity and Resistance to a given damage type becomes less common the higher it’s ranked. Most damage types have a **Condition** associated with them.

}}

#### Crushing Piercing and Slashing (Rank 1)
Crushing, Piercing, and Slashing together represent the ‘physical’ damage types. Most weapons deal one or more of these damage types, as do most physical hazards.

**Crushing** damage is dealt by anything that inflicts blunt-force trauma. Falling from high place, getting hit with a hammer, and being squeezed by a giant snake are all examples of Crushing damage. Rarely, Crushing damage can inflict the Bruised condition.

**Piercing** damage is dealt by anything that goes into its target. Arrows, rapiers, spike traps, and fangs all deal Piercing damage. Rarely, Piercing damage can cause a target to lose *Vitality*.

**Slashing** damage is dealt by anything that cuts the target, such as a sword, claw, or other edged hazard. Rarely, Slashing damage can inflict the Bleeding condition.

#### Arctic and Thermic (Rank 2)
Arctic and Thermic damage represent damage dealt by the transfer of kinetic energy.

**Arctic** damage is dealt by anything that rapidly removes heat, such as Ice or Frost spells. Environments of extreme cold will likely contain hazards that deal Arctic damage as well. Arctic damage often inflicts the Freezing condition.

**Thermic** damage is dealt by anything that rapidly adds heat, like Fire or Flame spells. Hazards involving extreme heat, such as pools of lava, also deal Thermic damage. Thermic damage often inflicts the Burning condition.

#### Psychic and Somatic (Rank 3)
Psychic and Somatic damage affect the soul and flesh of a creature, respectively. Anything that lacks a soul is immune to Psychic damage, while anything inorganic is immune to Somatic damage.

**Psychic** damage is dealt by effects that attack the mind or spirit of a creature, such as Ethereal creatures or telepathic attacks.

**Somatic** damage is dealt by anything that directly compromises flesh, such as poison and blood loss. Effects that wither flesh also deal Somatic damage.

\column

#### Caustic and Galvanic (Rank 4)
Caustic and Galvanic damage represent the subtraction and addition of matter at a molecular level.

**Caustic** damage is dealt by anything that physically dissolves material. Being drenched in acid or a highly basic solution would both deal caustic damage, usually with the Dissolving condition.

**Galvanic** damage is dealt when there is a massive transfer of electrons into the target, most often with lightning. Galvanic damage can rarely cause the Paralyzed condition.

#### Cosmic, Chthonic, and Sonic (Rank 5)
Cosmic, Chthonic, and Sonic damage represent the philosophical concepts of all that is above, below, and about, respectively. They are expressions of the fundamental forces that make up the Prismos.

**Cosmic** damage is dealt by exposure to unfathomable truths. Gaining forbidden knowledge, seeing the unseeable, and other eldritch events can induce this damage type. Cosmic damage often inflicts the Morphing condition.

**Chthonic** damage is dealt by exposure to the chaotic presence of the underworld. Being supernaturally subjugated, defaulting on an underworld deal, and the general presence of Infernal Light all deal Chthonic damage, which can also inflict the Possessed Condition.

**Sonic** damage is dealt by intense material force. The pressure wave of an explosion, conjurations of Temporal Light, and highly-magnified sound waves are all sources of Sonic damage. Sonic damage often inflicts the Stunned Condition.

#### Divine and Profane (Rank 6)
Divine and Profane damage are the rarest damage types, representing the enforcement and corruption of universal law, respectively.

**Divine** damage is dealt by effects utilizing Platinum Light to rebuke the very existence of their target. Divine damage can induce a variety of Conditions, though Blinded is common.

**Profane** damage is dealt from powers granted by elder beings outside the Prismos, corrupting the form and soul of their target. Profane damage can also cause many Conditions, though lose of *Vitality* is common.

}}

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Conditions
Many effects will leave their target with a **Condition**, which is a temporary, negative effect. Conditions sometimes require their triggering effect to state a duration, and sometimes list how they end (or worsen) in their definition.

### Condition Level & Multiple Instances
Some Conditions have a level (noted with an X by their name) which increases the potency of the condition. When a creature is afflicted with a Condition it already has, it takes the higher level between the two. Condition levels are never added together.

For example, if a creature is Burning 3 and gets hit by a Fire spell that inflicts Burning 1, it is still only Burning 3. If the creature was Burning 1 and the spell inflicted Burning 3, it would become Burning 3.

### Condition List

#### Bleeding X
You take Somatic damage at the start of each of your turns equal to your level times X. You can spend 5 $[AP] on your turn to reduce the level of this Condition by 1 if you have a bandage or other suitable item, as determined by the PM.

Immunity to Slashing damage prevents this condition.

#### Blinded
You can’t see, and suffer the effects below.

:

{{left
**Attack Checks** :: Attack Checks against you have Advantage.
**$[PER] Checks** :: Your $[PER] Checks have Disadvantage, and you suffer an automatic Complete Failure on any $[PER] Check entirely based on sight, as determined by the PM.
**Terrain** :: You treat all spaces as Treacherous Terrain.
**Visual Immunity** :: You are immune to any effect that requires the target to see it.
}}

#### Bruised
Your flesh is bruised. Your $[VIG] and $[AGI] Checks are Weakened until you finish a Rest.

Immunity to Crushing damage prevents this condition.

#### Buffeted X
Your AC is reduced by X. This condition ends at the start of your turn.

\column

#### Burning X
You are on fire. You are Numbed X and take 3X Thermic damage when you gain this Condition and at the start of your turn. You and any creature that can touch you may spend 2 $[AP] on their turn to reduce the level of this Condition by 1. This Condition ends automatically if you are ever submerged in water.

Immunity to Thermic damage prevents this Condition.

#### Deafened
You can’t hear, and suffer the effects below.

:

{{left
**$[PER] Checks** :: Your $[PER] Checks are Weakened, and you suffer an automatic Complete Failure on any $[PER] Check that depends entirely on hearing, as determined by the PM.
**Audible Immunity** :: You are immune to any effect that requires hearing to affect a target.
}}

#### Dissolving X
You are covered in a caustic substance. You take 2X Caustic damage when you gain this Condition and at the start of your turn. The level of this Condition reduces by 1 at the end of your turn. You and any creature that can touch you may spend 1 $[AP] on their turn to wipe off the substance with cloth or pour non-caustic liquid on you to reduce the level of this Condition by 1. It ends automatically if you are ever submerged in water.

Immunity to Caustic damage prevents this Condition.

#### Dulled X
Your $[PRS] Checks are Hindered X.

#### Dying X
You are dying. You take 2X Somatic damage at the start of your turn if your *Endurance* is above 0 (Somatic Resistance cannot reduce this damage below 1). You and any creature that can touch you may attempt to Stabilize you on their turn to reduce the level of this Condition. It ends automatically when you regain *Vitality*.

#### Exhausted X
You are extremely tired, and suffer the following effects:

:

{{left
**Hindered** :: All of your Checks are Hindered X.
**Sloppy** :: Attack Checks against you are Bolstered X.
**Slow** :: Your *Movement* is reduced by X (this stacks with the Slowed Condition).
}}

The level of this Condition reduces by 1 each time you finish a Long Rest.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Freezing X
You are extremely cold. You are Sluggish X and Slowed 2X. The level of this Condition reduces by 1 at the end of your turn. It also reduces by 1 each time you take Thermic damage.

Immunity to Arctic damage prevents this condition.

#### Immobilized
You can't move. Your $[AGI] Save Checks are Weakened, and all your *Movements* are 0. If you are Immobilized because of something physically holding you in place, anything that tries to move you by force must make a Check against a DC determined by what is Immobilizing you. The PM determines the Check and DC.

#### Morphing X
You are changing. You are Dulled 1, Muddled 1, and take 3X Cosmic damage at the start of your turn. At the end of your turn, make an $[INT] Save Check against a DC of 10 plus X.

:

{{left
**Success** :: The level of this Condition reduces by 2.
**Complete Success** :: The level of this Condition reduces by 3.
**Failure** :: Nothing.
**Deadly Failure** :: The level of this Condition increases by 1.
}}

The level of this Condition also reduces by 1 each time you take Sonic damage.

#### Muddled X
Your $[INT] Checks are Hindered X.

#### Numbed X
Your $[PER] Checks are Hindered X.

#### Pacified X
Your Attack and DC Checks are Hindered X.

#### Panicked X
You are overwhelmed with fear, and suffer the below effects.

:

{{left
**Frightened** :: You cannot move toward the source of your panic.
**Overwhelmed** :: You are Muddled X and Numbed X.
**Flight** :: When you gain this Condition, you immediately Stride as a Reaction and use all of your *Movement* to flee from the source of your panic.
}}

At the start of your turn you must make a $[PRS] Save with a DC of 10 plus X.

\column


{{left
**Success** :: The level of this condition reduces by 2.
**Complete Success** :: The level of this condition reduces by 3.
**Failure** :: You must Stride and use all of your *Movement* to flee from the source of your panic.
**Deadly Failure** :: As a Failure, but you must Stride twice, and the level of this Condition increases by 1.
}}

#### Paralyzed X
You are paralyzed, and suffer the below effects.

:

{{left
**No Actions** :: You lose all your $[AP] and can’t regain $[AP].
**Restrained** :: You are Restrained.
**Vulnerable** :: Your $[AGI] and $[PER] Checks are automatically Complete Failures.
}}

When you start your turn, your turn ends immediately, you don’t regain any $[AP], and the level of this Condition reduces by 1.

#### Poisoned X
You are poisoned. You take X Somatic damage at the start of your turn. You are also Sickened half of X, rounded down.

Every minute, make a $[VIG] Save Check against a DC of 10 plus X. On a Success, you stop taking damage from this Condition unless you gain it again.

Immunity to Somatic damage prevents this Condition.

#### Possessed X
Your mind is taken by chaotic forces, and you suffer the below effects.

:

{{left
**Frenzy** :: You must make at least one Attack Check on your turn, and any Attacks you make must target one of the creatures closest to you, chosen at random.
**Madness** :: You can only cast spells that costs 3 $[AP] or less and deal damage.
**Psychosis** :: You take 2X Infernal damage at the start of your turn.
}}

At the end of your turn, make a $[PRS] Save Check against a DC of 10 plus X.

:

{{left
**Success** :: The level of this Condition reduces by 2.
**Complete Success** :: The level of this Condition reduces by 3.
**Failure** :: Nothing.
**Deadly Failure** :: The level of this Condition increases by 1.
}}

The level of this Condition also reduces by 1 each time you take Sonic damage. It ends if you fall Unconscious.

#### Prone
You are laying flat. Attack Checks made against you have Advantage if the attacking entity is within 5 spaces of you. Otherwise they have Disadvantage.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Restrained
The movement of your limbs is restricted, and you suffer the below effects.

:

{{left
**Open** :: Attack Checks against you have Advantage (and are Bolstered 1 from being Seized).
**Restricted** :: You can’t take Move or Manipulate Actions except Escape. The PM may allow you to make Unarmed Strikes with unrestrained body parts such as your head at the cost of *Endurance*.
**Seized** :: You are Seized.
**Stuck** :: Your $[AGI] Checks have Disadvantage, and your $[AGI] Save Checks are automatic Deadly Failures.
}}

#### Seized
Something is holding you in place. You are Immobilized, Attack Checks against you are Bolstered 1, and Checks you make for Manipulate Actions are Hindered 1.

#### Sickened X
Your $[VIG] Checks are Hindered X.

#### Slowed X
Your Movement is reduced by X.

#### Sluggish X
Your $[AGI] Checks are Hindered X.

#### Stunned X
You are momentarily stunned. Any Reaction you take costs an additional X $[AP]. During your turn, you can't take Actions, but you can spend $[AP] to reduce the level of this Condition by 1 per $[AP] spent. 

When you start your turn Stunned, make an $[INT] Save Check against a DC of 10 plus X if you have an active Sustained effect. On a Failure, you can't Sustain this turn.

#### Unconscious
You are unconscious, and suffer the below effects.

{{left
**Incapacitated** :: You can’t spend $[AP].
**Vulnerable** :: Attack Checks against you are Bolstered 10, your $[AGI] and $[PER] Checks are automatic Complete or Deadly Failures, and all of your other Checks are Weakened.
}}

This Condition ends if you take damage, unless a specific effect is keeping you Unconscious.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

{{wide,text-align:center

# Player Characters

}}

## Character Creation
When creating a PC (Player Character) in BtP, you'll need to do each of the following things, any of which can serve as a starting point for the character creation process.

:

* Envision your character

* Determine your Attributes.

* Choose your **Ancestry**.

* Choose your **Background**.

* Choose your **Class**.

:

### Envisioning Your Character 
Sometimes you have an idea for your character's theme or personality before you know anything else about them. You may want to play a scientist, or a gruff bartender-turned-vigilante, or maybe you just want to be 'the strong man'. Maybe you want to be a strong bartender that used to be a scientist but after 'the accident' they stick to mixing drinks.

Whatever your idea, BtP is collaborative story telling game above all else. Your PC's ancestry, background, and class all serve to fulfill the vision you have for them, so take a moment to think through the kind of character you'll enjoy playing most. As you go along, work with your PM to find ways of fitting your PC's life into the story. Whether that means coming in as a complete stranger or having your backstory be a central plot thread, you should both work to make something that is, above all, fun.

### Choosing an Ancestry
You may want to start with your PC's **Ancestry**, which is their genetic heritage. There are many sapient species across the facets, such as humans or praedans, each of which come with their own traits. Your PM may decide that not all Ancestries are available to select, so work with them to choose one that best fits your character within the story being told.

### Choosing a Background
Your PC's **Background** represents their life before they became an adventurer, and is comprised of the following.

* Two Hobbies at Amateur Rank.

* One Profession at Proficient Rank.

* One Heritage Facet.

\column

Hobbies and Professions represent the skills your character has acquired up to the point that they became an adventurer, and are used to make Skill Checks other characters may not be able to, as described in the Hobbies & Professions section of this document. Your Heritage Facet represents the advantages gained from the environment they were raised in, be it a loving home with modest income or a brutal war camp where every day was a struggle.

The default suggestion for making a character in BtP is to make your own Background by picking your Hobbies and Profession, then choosing your Heritage Facet. Some pre-made Backgrounds are listed further in this document for inspiration or easy selection.

### Choosing a Class
The most impactful decision you will make toward fulfilling the vision of your character is their **Class**. Your PC's class represents the years of effort or exceptional natural endowment that grants them mind or might above other sapients. It will determine their role in your adventure, their abilities, and how they solve problems. You will generally want to discuss class selections with your fellow players and PM to create a balanced party.

### Assigning Attributes
Some players like to assign Attribute Points first and work up from there. There are two methods for determining your starting Attribute Points. The PM decides which method all the players will use.

#### Point Buy
With Point Buy, you have 12 Attribute Points to distribute between your $[VIG], $[AGI], $[PER], $[INT], and $[PRS], all of which begin at -1 points. At 1st level, a PC's Attributes can't go above 3 points (you can put no more than 4 Points into any one Attribute). Once you’ve decided how to spread your points, mark down the Mod Die for each of your Attributes, as explained in the Attributes section of this document.

#### Rolling
To roll for your Attribute Points, roll 5d4. Add the highest four values together, then add 1 to the total. You then assign Attribute Points as you would with Point Buy, using the final amount instead of 12.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

For maximum variability, you can also roll 1d4, subtract 1, use that number as your total $[VIG] Points, then do the same for your remaining Attributes.

### Alignment
In BtP, **Alignment** is not a measure of morality. Rather, Alignment measures your PC's resonance with the individual forces of creation, or Lights, described below.

:

{{color:darkorange **Flame** }} Light is evoked from the heated forges of civilization, fearless ingenuity, and the grit of a soldier.

:

{{color:cyan **Frost** }} Light permeates the quiet night air, the icy winds of mountain forests, and the patient gait of a hunter.

:

{{color:magenta **Pneuma** }} Light forms the thoughts of sapient creatures, interpersonal connection, and souls of all manner.

:

{{color:aquamarine **Soma** }} Light fuels the flesh of every living creature, from the strength of their muscles to their sensory instincts.

:

{{color:yellow **Mordant** }} Light suffuses the salted waves of the ocean, where selfish greed is the only way to survive.

:

{{color:violet **Velvet** }} Light is thunder and passion, bringing lovers together like lightning between storm clouds.

:

{{color:red **Infernal** }} Light is the fundamental force of power, radiating from the blood of those who let nothing stand in their way.

:

{{color:royalblue **Lunar** }} Light is all that is known, and all that is not. It is information, deception, and the never-ending cosmic expanse. Every mystery, and every answer.

:

{{color:lightgreen **Temporal** }} Light is the foundation of all that is real. It is the physicality of all things, the balancing force of existence, and the source of life in the Prismos.

:

**Platinum** Light is the very essence of creation, pure and unbiased. Ever-shining from the Sun at the apex of the Prismos, all other Lights refract from it.

#### Gaining Alignment
Your PC will gain levels of Alignment to different Lights from their ancestry, class, and their own personality. They may gain further levels throughout the course of the story.

Your PC's Alignment to each Light is measured in levels, shown in the table below.

::

##### Alignment Levels
:
| Level | Alignment |
|:-----:|:---------:|
| 0     | None      |
| 1     | Weak      |
| 2     | Firm      |
| 3     | Strong    |

\column

#### Personal Alignment
As you create your PC, determine your answer to each of the questions below. Your answers will grant you one level of Alignment as shown in the table further below.

Your answers to these questions will also live on your character sheet as a role-playing tool to quickly center yourself into your PC's personality. If you aren't sure how they would respond to something in the story, check your answers!

:

#### {{color:white I Respond to New... }}

:

**Situations with...**
{{color:darkorange **Action** }} / {{color:cyan **Patience** }}

:

**Problems with...**
{{color:magenta **Reason** }} / {{color:aquamarine **Instinct** }}

:

**People with...**
{{color:yellow **Apathy** }} / {{color:violet **Empathy** }}

::

| Situations | Problems | People  | Alignment |
|:-----------|:---------|:--------|:----------|
| Action     | Reason   | Apathy  | Flame     |
| Action     | Reason   | Empathy | Velvet    |
| Action     | Instinct | Apathy  | Mordant   |
| Action     | Instinct | Empathy | Any Light |
| Patience   | Reason   | Apathy  | Platinum  |
| Patience   | Reason   | Empathy | Pneuma    |
| Patience   | Instinct | Apathy  | Soma      |
| Patience   | Instinct | Empathy | Frost     |

::

#### Attunement
Alignment isn't just a cosmetic personality trait. Certain items imbued with Light, such as a gilded sword inscribed with Fire runes, are known as **Arcane** or **Supernatural** items, depending on how they were imbued.

Your character's Alignment will determine the Arcane and Supernatural items your character is able to Attune with. A PC can Attune with any items that match one of their Alignment types, and the strength of their Alignment improves the effects of such items when used.

#### Age
While your PC can technically be any age, it's recommended only experienced TTRPG players attempt to play a PC younger than 17, unless the player themselves is in that age range.

Otherwise, your PC can be as old as your PM allows. You can simply pick an age and move on, or you can intentionally age-up your character by 10 years to gain an additional two Hobbies and one Profession at Proficient Rank for the cost of 1 $[VIG], $[AGI], or $[PER] Point. You can repeat this process as many times as you like, but it’s not recommended to do so more than twice.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

### Devotion
The Prismatic Pantheon consists of ten gods, each of which govern a different Alignment. A description of each is provided below, along with some more esoteric entities, should you wish to play a character devout to a higher being.

#### {{color:white Primadeus }}
Creator god of the Prismos and the source of all Platinum Light. They are depicted as a many-winged, never-ending serpent with a  scaled lion's head. Primadeus maintains a neutral disposition, only truly enforcing one thing - that 1 prim is never worth more or less than 1 prim.

#### {{color:red Asmodeus }}
Patron god of the ori and the nexus of Infernal Light, often preferring the form of an imposing ori with up-turned horns that are symbolic in ori culture. He both is and is lord of the Infernal Prism, granting favor to the powerful, and power to those who take it.

#### {{color:lightgreen Temporia }}
Matriarch of the gods and the mundalves, she is the World Prism itself. Her Temporal Light imbues the land with life, and her followers seek to keep harmony among its creatures. With Asmodeus, she gave birth to Mordax and Aurodeus.

#### {{color:royalblue Lunaeus }}
Ever-watching enigma from the nadir of the Prismos, and progenitor of the ulu. He is, or is thought to be, the moon, shining Lunar Light upon the facets and rewarding those who seek the unknown. He's also an incorrigible trickster, prodding his followers into mischief. With Temporia, he sired Nixoria and Somaria.

#### {{color:yellow Mordax }}
Filled with hatred for his parents and apathy for almost all of his family, including his daxan spawn, Mordax is the god of spite. His Mordant Light reflects across the ocean waters and in the eyes of those with an axe to grind. His mother's approach brings the Drowning season, said to be her tears at his rejection.

#### {{color:violet Velestria }}
Blessed and loved child of Nixoria and Pneumax, often depicted as a voluptuous woman of the beholder's ancestry scantily clad in flowers. Mother of the amorous taurtons, her Velvet Light fills the cracking clouds of thunderstorms and the wombs of expectant, and attempting, mothers across the facets.

\column

#### {{color:aquamarine Somaria }}
Raging twin of the Wild Gods and Great Chiefmother of the corpug tribes. She is the matron of brute force, described as a giantess of rippling muscle clothed in animal pelts. She unlocks the primal potential of mortal flesh with blessings of Soma Light upon warriors who let nothing stop their victory.

#### {{color:magenta Pneumax }}
Guide to both the souls and thoughts of sapient creatures, and father of the cogitani. He was born of Asmodeus' jealous clash with Lunaeus, and brokered peace among the Prismatic Pantheon. His ethereal form permeates the the Soulscape, connecting people with rays of Pneuma Light.

#### {{color:darkorange Aurodeus }}
The Daybringer, who forged humans in the ambition of his Flame Light. He grants resolve to the bold-hearted, and fuels the fires of civilization. Temporia visits him each year, bringing the Gold Season.

#### {{color:cyan Nixoria }}
Silent twin of the Wild Gods and Nightmother of the praedan. She steps in tandem with the footfalls of hunters, is warden to the wilderness of the World Prism, and her Frost Light caresses the weary into gentle sleep. She welcomes her mother each year for the Silver Season.

#### {{color:crimson Gïgeye }}
It is said the shadows can hold many terrors, but none more terrifying than the vampyre. Their obluminous lineage is a mystery, but profaned scholars claim in a time before the severing, a gigantic mass of congealed blood consumed a pregnant ori, infecting her child with the first strain of vampyrism.

#### {{color:black Ukthulus }}
All that is, is Light. What then, of darkness? In the Void, there can be nothing but death eternal. It cannot be traveled through, as there is nothing to walk upon. How then, does a dead god stir within its umbra?

#### {{color:darkgray The Lady }}
What begins, must inevitably end. They did not go unnoticed. Their time will come. She is waiting. She is near.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

### Filling in Your Sheet
Once you have a firm understanding of the PC you're going to play, its time to fill in your character sheet.
:
[Link to Character Sheet (Google Sheets)](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1aN_r7_c4Xdz_y_rZrbsLOnsnjLVIUSapcuDi4eTeKu4/edit?usp=sharing)
:
Follow the guide below to calculate your PC's stats.

#### Step 1. Fill in Your Attributes
Whether rolling or using point buy, assign your attribute points.

##### $[VIG] Vigor
:
- ***Bulk.*** With each $[VIG] Point, you unlock the matching Bonus Inventory row.

- ***Jump Distance.*** Your Max Long Jump distance equals 2 plus your $[VIG] Points. Your Max High Jump height equals 1 plus half your $[VIG] Points, rounded down.
- ***Vitality Maximum.*** For every 2 Points, increase your Vitality Maximum by 1.
:
##### $[AGI] Agility
:
- ***Movement.*** Your base *Movement* is 3. Increase it by your $[AGI] Points each Point. This means if you have 1 $[AGI] Point, increase it by 1. If you have 2, increase it by 1, then 2, and so on.
:
##### $[PER] Perception
:
- ***Initiative.*** Set your Initiative to your $[PER] Mod Die.

- ***Perception Radius.*** Your base $[PER] Radius is 5. Increase it by your $[PER] Points each Point, as with $[AGI] and *Movement*.
:
##### $[INT] Intelligence
:
- ***Hobbies.*** For each $[INT] Point you have, you gain one Hobby at Amateur Rank.

- ***Professions.*** For every 2 Points, you gain one Profession at Proficient Rank.
:
##### $[PRS] Presence
:
- ***Social Hobbies.*** For each $[PRS] Point you have, you gain one Hobby involving communication with other people at Amateur Rank.

- ***Radiance.*** For every 2 Points, your total *Radiance* increases by 1.

#### Step 2. Ancestry Facets
Write down your ancestry, then go over the facets it grants. Some will further increase your *Movement*, or grant additional *Vitality* or *Radiance*. All ancestries will grant you a level of Alignment. Make these changes to your sheet, and if you'd like you can write down your ancestry facets in the Facets section of your sheet.

\column

#### Step 3. Background
Add the Hobbies from your Background at Amateur Rank, and the Profession at Proficient Rank. Then write down your Heritage Facet in the Facets section of your sheet and apply any bonuses  you gained from it to your statistics.

#### Step 4. Class
Your class will make the biggest changes to your sheet. Write down your chosen class in the "Class" box, then address the following.

##### Alignment
Add the level of Alignment gained from your class, if applicable.

##### Armor & Weapons
In the Armor Training and Weapon Training sections on your sheet, note the types of armor and weapons your character can use.

##### Endurance Maximum
Set your class's starting *Endurance* as your Endurance Maximum.

##### Save Checks
In the Save Checks section on your sheet, note which Save Checks can be rolled in Tandem with your Class Mode Die. Use a "/" between the Mod Dice for Tandem rolls, or a "+" if the Save Check is Empowered, as with an arcanist's $[INT] Save Checks.

##### Facets & Actions
In the Facets section of your sheet, write down all of your class's 1st level facets. in the Actions section, write down any special actions you received from your class, with their $[AP] cost and a brief description.

##### Spells & Maneuvers
If you gained any spells or maneuvers at 1st level, write them down in that section of your sheet, along with the resource to use them. You will keep track of that resource in that section.

##### AC (Armor Class)
While not wearing armor, your AC equals the **sum of your highest two Attributes**. If you have armor at the start of the story, calculate your AC with the rules in the Equipment section of this document, including your Class Bonus if allowed by your class.

#### Step 5. Final Details
Using the rules earlier in this chapter, answer the questions that define your PC's personal Alignment. Decide if you would like to age-up your character, and if you would like to follow a god. You can also work with your PM to create friends, rivals, or even enemies that connect you to the world of the story being told.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

## Ancestries
Below is the list of Ancestries available for players to choose from. If you would like your PC to look different from the standard options, Alignment can often affect the color of a creatures eyes, hair, or even skin. For example, an ori with Flame Alignment could have vibrant orange or gold eyes.

### Cogitani
Brilliant from birth, cogitani are bastions of reason and critical thinking.

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:-----------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Any shade of red, magenta, or blue; $[INT] shaped pupils |
| **Hair**   | Navy blue, dark purple, or plum    |
| **Height** | 4 to 5 feet on average             |
| **Skin**   | Light to dark magenta or plum      |

::

##### Distinct Features
The cogitani sport a short horn with a wide base on their forehead. The horn is a special sensory organ that can detect information from the Soulscape. Some cogitani even develop psionic abilities that are channeled through it. The horn's texture is genetic, often hinting at their family lineage.


#### Facets
:
***Brilliant Soul.*** You gain a level of Pneuma Alignment.
:
***Resilient Mind.*** You have Psychic Resistance equal to your Class Bonus.
:
***Vibrant Thought.*** Your $[INT] Save and Skill Checks are Bolstered 1.

### Corpug
Tough as nails and just as pointy, the corpug are powerhouses that outperform their stature.
:

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:----------------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Any shade of green, teal, or cyan; $[PER] shaped pupils |
| **Hair**   | Any color from grass green to cyan      |
| **Height** | 4 to 5 feet on average                  |
| **Skin**   | Dark teal to turquoise                  |

::

##### Distinct Features
Short, sturdy bone spikes protrude from every one of a corpug's joints, giving them a wicked punch, and an even worse elbow.

#### Facets
:
***Ferocious Soul.*** You gain a level of Soma Alignment.
:
***Grit.*** Your $[VIG] Save Checks are Bolstered 1.

\column

***Powerful Build.*** The Bulk Rank of items you carry on your person (not in a backpack) is decreased by 1 (minimum 5). Both DV's of your Unarmed Strikes also get a bonus equal to your $[VIG] points.
:
***Resilient Body.*** You have Somatic Resistance equal to your Class Bonus.

### Daxan
Slime-Scaled scoundrels of the scoured shores, daxans find home in places others would dissolve.

:

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:-------------------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Green to yellow; triangular pupils pointed outward |
| **Hair**   | None                                       |
| **Height** | 5 to 6 feet on average                     |
| **Scales** | Bright yellow to dull chartreuse           |

::

##### Distinct Features
Daxans have rigid eyebrows and outer lips incapable of forming facial expressions, like a lizard. They speak through a set of internal lips behind their teeth. They can use their mouth and claws as a weapon.

#### Facets
:
***Salty Soul.*** You gain a level of Mordant Alignment.
:
***Acidic Adaptation.*** You have Caustic Resistance equal to your Class Bonus.
:
***Oceanic.*** You can breathe both air and water, and you have *Swim Movement* equal to your *Movement*. You can also drink salt water as though it were fresh water.
:
***Claw and Fang.*** Your claws and maw are natural weapons. When you Unarmed Strike with one of them, the DV equals half your Class Bonus and the Deadly DV equals your Class Bonus. When you Unarmed Strike with your claws, they deal Slashing damage and have the Light property (you have two). When you Unarmed Strike with your maw, it deals Piercing damage and has the Pulling property.
:
***Night Vision.*** You have Night Vision.

### Human
Multifaceted and ever adapting, humans are emblematic of the resilience of civilization.
:

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:------------------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Any shade of brown, green, orange, or red |
| **Hair**   | Auburn, blonde, or brown                  |
| **Height** | 5 1/2 to 6 1/2 feet on average            |
| **Skin**   | Any shade of wheat                        |

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

#### Facets
:
***Flaming Soul.*** You gain a level of Flame Alignment.
:
***Bonus Attribute Point.*** You gain an additional Attribute Point.
:
***Extra Hobbies.*** You gain two additional Hobbies at Novice Rank.
:
***Radiant.*** You gain an additional *Radiance*, and you regain 1 spent *Radiance* when you finish a Rest.

### Mundalf
Stewards of the earth with bodies stout as stone, mundalves are the born rulers of the Undergrowth.
:

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:-------------------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Any shade of green; diamond pupils         |
| **Hair**   | White, pale or dark green, or brown        |
| **Height** | 3 1/2 to 4 1/2 feet on average             |
| **Skin**   | Color and pattern of any stone             |

::

##### Distinct Features
Mundalves are short. incredibly stocky, and almost always bearded. Their skin is not just colored but textured after different kinds of stone, showing their region of origin in the Undergrowth.

#### Facets
:
***Stone Soul.*** You gain a level of Temporal Alignment.
:
***Extra Endurance.*** Your Endurance Maximum gains a bonus equal to twice your Level.
:
***Extra Vitality.*** You gain an additional *Vitality*.
:
***Mundalven Resilience.*** You have Crushing Resistance equal to your Class Bonus and Stunned Resistance equal to half your Class Bonus.
:
***Night Vision.*** You have Night Vision.

### Ori
Resolute to the end, the ori are a proud and powerful people, each born to lord their own domain.
:

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:----------------------------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Black, pink, or red; triangular pupils pointed down |
| **Hair**   | Black, or any shade of red from pale pink to black  |
| **Height** | 5 to 6 feet on average                              |
| **Skin**   | Reddish-brown to crimson or pale pink                |

::

##### Distinct Features
Two horns slope downward from an ori's temples, like an upside down bull. The size and shape of these horns are inherited from their family lineage. Their earlobes are long, rigid and point downward as well.

\column

#### Facets
:
***Powerful Soul.*** You gain a level of Infernal Alignment.
:
***Blood Frenzy.*** The first time you Stride during your turn, if you end your *Movement* with a Hostile creature in reach of your weapon, you regain 1 spent $[AP].
:
***Extra Endurance.*** Your Endurance Maximum gains a bonus equal to twice your Level.
:
***Unyielding.*** Your $[PRS] Save Checks are Bolstered 1, and you can make Survival Save Checks using $[PRS] instead of $[VIG].

### Praedan
Children of the Nightmother in all their various forms, praedan are as varied as the beasts they resemble.

:

#### Appearance

::

|  |  |
|:-------|:-------------------------------------------------------|
| **Eyes**   | Any shade of cyan, blue, brown, or green               |
| **Fur**    | Typically black, brown, or white, based on environment |
| **Height** | 4 to 7 feet on average                                 |

::

##### Distinct Features
Praedans resemble anthropomorphic apex predators, such as bears or cats. This gives them highly variable features between their different tribes.

#### Facets
:
***Wild Soul.*** You gain a level of Frost Alignment.
:
***Nightblooded.*** You have Arctic Resistance equal to your Class Bonus.
:
***Night Vision.*** You have Night Vision.
:
***Keen Senses.*** Your $[PER] Attribute Checks are Bolstered 1.
:
***Sub-Ancestry.*** Choose **Canine**, **Feline**, **Pinnipine**, or **Ursine** as your praedan sub-ancestry.

:

##### Canine
As a canine praedan, your features resemble those of a dog, wolf, or other canid. You gain the following facets.
:
***Extra Endurance.*** Your Endurance Maximum gains a bonus equal to twice your Level.
:
***Extra Hobbies.*** You gain two additional Hobbies at Novice Rank.
:
***Fast.*** Your *Movement* increases by 1 space.

##### Feline
As a feline praedan, your features resemble those of a cat. You gain the following facets.
:
***Claws.*** Your hands have claws that are natural weapons. They deal Slashing damage and have the Pulling property. When you Unarmed Strike with them, the DV equals half your Class Bonus and the Deadly DV equals your Class Bonus.
:
***Climber.*** While nothing is covering your claws, you have *Climb Movement* equal to your *Movement*.
:
***Fast.*** Your *Movement* increases by 1 space.

:

##### Pinnipine
As a pinnipine praedan, your features resemble those of seals or walruses. You gain the following facets.
:
***Diver.*** You have *Swim Movement* equal to your *Movement* and can hold your breath for up to an hour.
:
***Blubbery.*** You have Crushing Resistance equal to your Class Bonus and Freezing Resistance equal to half your Class Bonus.

:

##### Ursine
As an ursine praedan, your features resemble those of the mighty bear. You gain the following facets.
:
***Bear Claws.*** Your hands have claws that are natural weapons. They deal Slashing damage and have the Brutal property. When you Unarmed Strike with them, the DV equals half your Class Bonus and the Deadly DV equals your Class Bonus.
:
***Extra Vitality.*** You gain an additional *Vitality*.
:
***Powerful Build.*** The Bulk Rank of items you carry on your person (not in a backpack) is decreased by 1 (minimum 5). Both DV’s of your Unarmed Strikes also get a bonus equal to your $[VIG] points, including with your Bear Claws.

### Soli
First of the sapients and children of Primadeus, the soli stand as regal arbiters of the World Prism.
:

#### Appearance

::

##### Distinct Features
The faces of Soli resemble a cross between a lion and a lizard. They are covered head to foot in vibrant, sturdy scales reflective of their Light lineage. Their hair grows like a mane down or around their head.

\column

#### Facets
:

:
***Sub-Ancestry.*** Choose one of the Alignments listed below as your soli sub-ancestry.

:

As a flame soli, your scales can range from vibrant orange to gold. You gain the following facets.

:

:
***Extra Radiance.*** You gain an additional *Radiance*.

:

##### Frost

:
***Frozen Soul.*** You gain a level of Frost Alignment.
:
***Cold Resistant.*** You have Arctic Resistance equal to your Class Bonus and Freezing Resistance equal to half your Class Bonus.
:
***Claws.*** Your hands have claws that are natural weapons. They deal Slashing damage and have the Precise property. When you Unarmed Strike with them, the DV equals half your Class Bonus and the Deadly DV equals your Class Bonus.

:

:

As a lunar soli, your scales can range from royal to navy blue. You gain the following facets.
:

:

##### Temporal
As temporal soli, your scales can be a spectrum of verdant greens. You gain the following facets.
:
***Temporal Soul.*** You gain a level of Temporal Alignment.
:

:

##### Platinum
As a platinum soli, your scales shine of polished, pure platinum metal. You gain the following facets.
:
***Pure Soul.*** You gain a level of Platinum Alignment.
:
***Extra Radiance.*** You gain an additional *Radiance*.
:
***Heaven’s Pull.*** You can hover just above the ground, giving you the benefits below.
:
- You can move over treacherous terrain less than 1 space high.

- You can move over bodies of liquid by spending 2 *Movement* for every 1 space you hover over.

- You get an automatic Complete Success against any Hostile effect that requires you to be on the ground, so long as nothing is holding you to it.

:

***Prismatic Resistance.*** You have Resistance to all damage types except Crushing, Piercing, or Slashing, equal to half your Class bonus.

Mysterious as the moon and just as silent, ulu leave those who behold them questioning their place in the universe.

Ulu are tall and slender, with ears that point sharply toward the sky, so much so the tips of their ears are almost level with the top of their head.

#### Facets
:

:
***Everwatching.*** You do not need to Sleep as part of a Long Rest. You can instead spend those 6 hours doing anything that isn't a Concentrate action.
:
***Gravity Warp.*** The maximum distance you can move when you Jump is doubled, and you can move 2 spaces for every 1 space you would move during your jump. When you fall, halve the number of spaces you fell before calculating fall damage.
:
***Lunar Gift.*** You learn one Moon cantrip or Lunar mantra of your choice. You can cast it as an Arcane Hostile Action by spending the $[AP] listed in the spell’s description. When cast this way, your eyes glow, and the spell has no hand or spoken requirement. Any Checks made as part of casting the spell count as $[INT] Checks.
:
***Night Vision.*** You have Night Vision.
:
***Telepathic.*** You learn the Transference cantrip, and can cast it as an Arcane Action. When you choose the Relay option, you can spend any amount of $[AP], instead of 5, and the message can be a number of seconds up to the $[AP] spent.

## Backgrounds
Below is a list of Backgrounds you can use for inspiration in making your own, or as is for your PC. The Profession gained from your Background is at Proficient Rank. The Hobbies are at Amateur Rank. The Heritage Facets listed in each background are defined in the next section.

You grew up with educational opportunities, and chose to expand your community.
:
**Profession** Architect
:
**Hobbies** Cooking, Nonfiction Reading
:
**Facet** Dabbler

You dedicated your life to helping people in their moment of need.
:
**Profession** Paramedic
:
**Hobbies** Carousing, Woodworking
:
**Facet** First Responder

You've made a living out of going where others can't.
:
**Profession** Trail Guide
:
**Hobbies** Hunting, Historical Fiction Reading
:
**Facet** Climber

You were born in the frying pan, and now tend its fire.
:
**Profession** Chef
:
**Hobbies** Dancing, Fishing
:
**Facet** Chef

For glory or gold, your place is on the field.
:
**Profession** Track Athlete
:
**Hobbies** Gambling, Gardening
:
**Facet** Speedster

No one handed you your life, you had to carve it with a bloody knife.
:

:
**Hobbies** Card Counting, Sleight-of-Hand

You've spent your life outdoors, both personal and professional.
:

:
**Hobbies** Bushcraft, Distilling
:

## Heritage Facets
Below are all the Heritage Facets available for you to take as part of your Background.

### Blue-Collar
You work with your hands. Both DVs of melee weapons you wield that lack the Deft and Precise properties increase by 1.

### Chef
You know your way around a pan. As part of a Short Rest, if you have the means to do so you can cook a meal that restores 1 spent *Radiance* if consumed immediately after. The meal can feed up to 6 creatures.

Once you use this facet, you must finish a Long Rest before you can do so agian.

### Climber
You're an adept mountaineer, giving you the benefits below.

:

**Climb Movement** You have *Climb Movement* equal to half your Movement.

:

**Extra Endurance** Your Endurance Maximum gains a bonus equal to twice your Level.

### Dabbler
Your favorite hobby is finding new hobbies. You gain three new Hobbies, each at Novice rank.

### First Responder
You're trained in crisis response, giving you the benefits below.

:

**Quick** Your Movement increases by 1.

:

**Unhesitatant** Your Initiative Checks are Bolstered 1.

### Hardened
You've lived horrors few would dream, giving you the benefits below.

:

**Intimidating** Skill Checks you make to intimidate another creature are Bolstered 1.

:

**Unphased** You have Panicked Resistance 1.

### Speedster
You're pretty fast. Your *Movement* increases by 3.

\column

## Experiences
Experiences are a form of player reward, given as a result of daring actions, successes, and failures. They are a way to reward players on the spot with a boon that calls back to their triumphs and lessons.

Experiences are usually a single-use boon that grants Advantage on a specific kind of Check, and are named after the success the player achieved. For example, a player that defeats a Large creature may receive the 'Monster Slayer' experience, granting Advantage on a single Attack Check against a Large creature.

Though they normally grant Advantage, the PM can award Experiences with any effect they desire, and can give it multiple uses. However, Experiences in general should not be permanent.

## Levels
As your PC advances through the story, they will learn and grow, eventually culminating in a Level Up. BtP doesn't have experience points, instead opting for a set of guidelines meant to foster an organic, sensible progression that avoids power spikes and showcases the character's growth over time. The table below shows the time characters should spend at each level across Exploration and Downtime before moving to the next level, but when PCs Level Up is ultimately the PM's decision. 

::

#### Time in Level
|Level|Time-in-Level|
|:-:|:-:|
|1|1 month|
|2|3 months|
|3|6 months|
|4|10 months|
|5|15 months|
|6|21 months|
|7|28 months|
|8|36 months|
|9|45 months|

::

The increasing time spent at each level is meant to give narrative weight to them. Reaching level 1 in a class represents either years of hard work and training or exceptional natural ability. If your PC encounters a 10th level Arcanist or Gladiator NPC at 1st level, that means they're about 14 years ahead of where you are, and have spent those 14 years rigorously perfecting their craft.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page

These timeframes also help enforce the kind of stories meant to be told at each level. Stories in Btp can be loosely split into three tiers: level 1-3, 4-6, and 7-9. Level 10 can either be used as a series climax, epilogue, or Bonus chapter, depending on the story.

Below is a table that shows the scale of threat a monster (or PC) is meant to represent at each level.

::

|Level|Threat to a...|
|:-:|:-:|
|-1|Small animal|
|0|Lone commoner|
|1|Group of commoners|
|2|Trained soldier|
|3|Group of soldiers|
|4|Village battalion|
|5|Township|
|6|Fortified settlement|
|7|Major city|
|8|Nation Capitol|
|9|Unified region|
|10|Country|
|11|Multiple countries|
|12|Continent|
|13|Plane of existence|
|14|Multiple planes|
|15|Minor deity|
|16|Outer realm|
|17|Major deity|
|18|Universe|
|19|Prime deity|
|20|Elder Titan|

::

Your PC should never encounter an enemy more than 4 levels above their own. So for stories told from level 1-3, your party will be dealing with threats to individuals, the local populace, and townships, usually within the span of one year. The scale of conflict ramps up from there, as will the stakes of your story and the consequences of your character's actions.

\column

## Leveling Up
When its time for your PC to level up, come back here and consult the Level Up Checklist below.

### Level Up Checklist
:
- ***Endurance.*** Increase your Endurance Maximum, as described in your class's Endurance section.

- ***Class Resources.*** Consult your class table to increase the size of your Class Die and Class Bonus, your available class resources, and the maximum level of your spells or maneuvers to those of your new level.

- ***Facets.*** Add the class facets of your new level to your sheet.

- ***Attributes.*** If you gained an Attribute Point from a facet, increase the relevant statistic or resource as described in step 1 of Filling in Your Sheet.

- ***Hobbies & Professions.*** If your new level is odd, increase the rank of one of your Hobbies or Professions (minding the Rank Cap), or become Novice in a new Hobby.

![](https://i.imgur.com/hd3lq4V.png){position:absolute,top:0,left:0,opacity:0.5,width:100%}
![](https://i.imgur.com/NQCPF0W.png){position:absolute,bottom:0.3%,left:0,width:100%}
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\page
